The default\n   228→`t:algs_list/0` can be obtained from `default_algorithms/0`.\n   229→\n   230→If an alg_entry() is missing in the algs_list(), the default value is used for\n   231→that entry.\n   232→\n   233→Here is an example of this option:\n   234→\n   235→```erlang\n   236→\t  {preferred_algorithms,\n   237→\t  [{public_key,['ssh-rsa','ssh-dss']},\n   238→\t  {cipher,[{client2server,['aes128-ctr']},\n   239→          {server2client,['aes128-cbc','3des-cbc']}]},\n   240→\t  {mac,['hmac-sha2-256','hmac-sha1']},\n   241→\t  {compression,[none,zlib]}\n   242→\t  ]\n   243→\t  }\n   244→```\n   245→\n   246→The example specifies different algorithms in the two directions (client2server\n   247→and server2client), for cipher but specifies the same algorithms for mac and\n   248→compression in both directions. The kex (key exchange) is implicit but\n   249→public_key is set explicitly.\n   250→\n   251→For background and more examples see the\n   252→[User's Guide](configure_algos.md#introduction).\n   253→\n   254→If an algorithm name occurs more than once in a list, the behaviour is\n   255→undefined. The tags in the property lists are also assumed to occur at most one\n   256→time.\n   257→\n   258→> #### Warning {: .warning }\n   259→>\n   260→> Changing the values can make a connection less secure. Do not change unless\n   261→> you know exactly what you are doing. If you do not understand the values then\n   262→> you are not supposed to change them.\n   263→\"\"\".\n   264→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   265→-type double_algs(AlgType)  :: list( {client2server,[AlgType]} | {server2client,[AlgType]} )\n   266→                             | [AlgType].\n   267→\n   268→-doc \"\"\"\n   269→Modifies the list of algorithms to use in the algorithm negotiation. The\n   270→modifications are applied after the option `preferred_algorithms` (if existing)\n   271→is applied.\n   272→\n   273→The algorithm for modifications works like this:\n   274→\n   275→- Input is the `t:modify_algs_list/0` and a set of algorithms `A` obtained from\n   276→  the `preferred_algorithms` option if existing, or else from the\n   277→  [ssh:default_algorithms/0](`default_algorithms/0`).\n   278→- The head of the `t:modify_algs_list/0` modifies `A` giving the result `A'`.\n   279→\n   280→  The possible modifications are:\n   281→\n   282→  - Append or prepend supported but not enabled algorithm(s) to the list of\n   283→    algorithms. If the wanted algorithms already are in `A` they will first be\n   284→    removed and then appended or prepended,\n   285→  - Remove (rm) one or more algorithms from `A`.\n   286→\n   287→- Repeat the modification step with the tail of `t:modify_algs_list/0` and the\n   288→  resulting `A'`.\n   289→\n   290→If an unsupported algorithm is in the `t:modify_algs_list/0`, it will be\n   291→silently ignored\n   292→\n   293→If there are more than one modify_algorithms options, the result is undefined.\n   294→\n   295→Here is an example of this option:\n   296→\n   297→```text\n   298→\t  {modify_algorithms,\n   299→\t  [{prepend, [{kex, ['diffie-hellman-group1-sha1']}],\n   300→\t  {rm,      [{compression, [none]}]}\n   301→\t  ]\n   302→\t  }\n   303→```\n   304→\n   305→The example specifies that:\n   306→\n   307→- the old key exchange algorithm 'diffie-hellman-group1-sha1' should be the main\n   308→  alternative. It will be the main alternative since it is prepened to the list\n   309→- The compression algorithm none (= no compression) is removed so compression is\n   310→  enforced\n   311→\n   312→For background and more examples see the\n   313→[User's Guide](configure_algos.md#introduction).\n   314→\"\"\".\n   315→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   316→-type modify_algs_list()      :: list( {append,algs_list()} | {prepend,algs_list()} | {rm,algs_list()} ) .\n   317→\n   318→-type internal_options()      :: ssh_options:private_options().\n   319→-type socket_options()        :: [gen_tcp:connect_option() | gen_tcp:listen_option()].\n   320→\n   321→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   322→-type client_options()        :: [ client_option() ] .\n   323→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   324→-type daemon_options()        :: [ daemon_option() ].\n   325→\n   326→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   327→-type common_options() :: [ common_option() ].\n   328→-doc \"\"\"\n   329→The options above can be used both in clients and in daemons (servers). Both the max time and max\n   391→amount of data could be configured:\n   392→\n   393→- `{Minutes, Bytes}` initiate rekeying when any of the limits are reached.\n   394→- `Bytes` initiate rekeying when `Bytes` number of bytes are transferred, or at\n   395→  latest after one hour.\n   396→\n   397→When a rekeying is done, both the timer and the byte counter are restarted.\n   398→Defaults to one hour and one GByte.\n   399→\n   400→If `Minutes` is set to `infinity`, no rekeying will ever occur due to that max\n   401→time has passed. Setting `Bytes` to `infinity` will inhibit rekeying after a\n   402→certain amount of data has been transferred. If the option value is set to\n   403→`{infinity, infinity}`, no rekeying will be initiated. Note that rekeying\n   404→initiated by the peer will still be performed.\n   405→\"\"\".\n   406→-doc(#{title => <<\"Common Options\">>}).\n   407→-type limit_time()  :: pos_integer() | infinity .\n   408→\n   409→-doc \"\"\"\n   410→Module implementing the behaviour `m:ssh_client_key_api` and/or\n   411→`m:ssh_server_key_api`. The\n   564→  default is to ask the user with a question on stdio of whether to accept or\n   565→  reject the new Host Key. See the option\n   566→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for specifying the path to\n   567→  the file `known_hosts` where previously accepted Host Keys are recorded. See\n   568→  also the option [key_cb](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) for the general way to\n   569→  handle keys.\n   570→\n   571→  The option can be given in three different forms as seen\n   572→  [above](`t:accept_hosts/0`):\n   573→\n   574→  - The value is a `t:boolean/0`. The value `true` will make the client accept\n   575→    any unknown Host Key without any user interaction. The value `false`\n   576→    preserves the default behaviour of asking the user on stdio.\n   577→  - An `t:accept_callback/0` will be called and the boolean return value `true`\n   578→    will make the client accept the Host Key. A return value of `false` will\n   579→    make the client to reject the Host Key and as a result the connection will\n   580→    be closed. The arguments to the fun are:\n   581→    - `PeerName` \\- a string with the name or address of the remote host.\n   582→    - `FingerPrint` \\- the fingerprint of the Host Key as\n   583→      `hostkey_fingerprint/1` calculates it.\n   584→  - A tuple `{HashAlgoSpec, accept_callback}`. The `HashAlgoSpec` specifies\n   585→    which hash algorithm shall be used to calculate the fingerprint used in the\n   586→    call of the `t:accept_callback/0`. The `HashALgoSpec` is either an atom or a\n   587→    list of atoms as the first argument in `hostkey_fingerprint/2`. If it is a\n   588→    list of hash algorithm names, the `FingerPrint` argument in the\n   589→    `t:accept_callback/0` will be a list of fingerprints in the same order as\n   590→    the corresponding name in the `HashAlgoSpec` list.\n   591→\n   592→- **`user_interaction`** - If `false`, disables the client to connect to the\n   593→  server if any user interaction is needed, such as accepting the server to be\n   594→  added to the `known_hosts` file, or supplying a password.\n   595→\n   596→  Even if user interaction is allowed it can be suppressed by other options,\n   597→  such as `silently_accept_hosts` and `password`. However, those options are not\n   598→  always desirable to use from a security point of view.\n   599→\n   600→  Defaults to `true`.\n   601→\n   602→- **`save_accepted_host`** - If `true`, the client saves an accepted host key to\n   603→  avoid the accept question the next time the same host is connected. If the\n   604→  option [`key_cb`](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) is not present, the key is saved\n   605→  in the file \"known_hosts\". See option\n   606→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for the location of that\n   607→  file.\n   608→\n   609→  If `false`, the key is not saved and the key will still be unknown at the next\n   610→  access of the same host.\n   611→\n   612→  Defaults to `true`\n   613→\n   614→- **`quiet_mode`** - If `true`, the client does not print anything on\n   615→  authorization.\n   616→\n   617→  Defaults to `false`\n   618→\"\"\".\n   619→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   620→-type host_accepting_client_options() ::\n   621→        {silently_accept_hosts, accept_hosts()}\n   622→      | {user_interaction,     boolean()}\n   623→      | {save_accepted_host,   boolean()}\n   624→      | {quiet_mode,           boolean()} .\n   625→\n   626→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   627→-type accept_hosts() :: boolean()\n   628→                      | accept_callback()\n   629→                      | {HashAlgoSpec::fp_digest_alg(), accept_callback()}.\n   630→\n   631→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   632→-type fp_digest_alg() :: 'md5' | crypto:sha1() | crypto:sha2() .\n   633→\n   634→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   635→-type accept_callback() :: fun((PeerName::string(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % Old style\n   636→                         | fun((PeerName::string(), Port::inet:port_number(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % New style\n   637→                           .\n   638→\n   639→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   640→-type fingerprint() :: string() | [string()].\n   641→\n   642→-doc \"\"\"\n   643→- **`user`** - Provides the username. The default is to use the Erlang shell: `{shell, start, []}`\n   740→\n   741→See the option [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) for a description of\n   742→how the daemon executes shell-requests and exec-requests depending on the shell-\n   743→and exec-options.\n   744→\"\"\".\n   745→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   746→-type 'shell_fun/2'() :: fun((User::string(),  PeerAddr::inet:ip_address()) -> pid()).\n   747→\n   748→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   749→-type exec_daemon_option()      :: {exec, exec_spec()} .\n   750→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   751→-type exec_spec()               :: {direct, exec_fun()} | disabled | deprecated_exec_opt().\n   752→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   753→-type exec_fun()                :: 'exec_fun/1'() | 'exec_fun/2'() | 'exec_fun/3'().\n   754→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   755→-type 'exec_fun/1'() :: fun((Cmd::string()) -> exec_result()) .\n   756→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   757→-type 'exec_fun/2'() :: fun((Cmd::string(), User::string()) -> exec_result()) .\n   758→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   759→-type 'exec_fun/3'() :: fun((Cmd::string(), User::string(), ClientAddr::ip_port()) -> exec_result()) .\n   760→-doc \"\"\"\n   761→This option changes how the daemon executes exec-requests from clients. The term\n   762→in the return value is formatted to a string if it is a non-string type. No\n   763→trailing newline is added in the ok-case.\n   764→\n   765→See the User's Guide section on\n   766→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   767→\n   768→Error texts are returned on channel-type 1 which usually is piped to `stderr` on\n   769→e.g Linux systems. Texts from a successful execution are returned on\n   770→channel-type 0 and will in similar manner be piped to `stdout`. The exit-status\n   771→code is set to 0 for success and 255 for errors. The exact results presented on\n   772→the client side depends on the client and the client's operating system.\n   773→\n   774→In case of the `{direct, exec_fun()}` variant or no exec-option at all, all\n   775→reads from `standard_input` will be from the received data-events of type 0.\n   776→Those are sent by the client. Similarly all writes to `standard_output` will be\n   777→sent as data-events to the client. An OS shell client like the command 'ssh'\n   778→will usually use stdin and stdout for the user interface.\n   779→\n   780→The option cooperates with the daemon-option\n   781→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) in the following way:\n   782→\n   783→- **1\\. If neither the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) nor the\n   784→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present:** - The default Erlang\n   785→  evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result is returned to\n   786→  the client.\n   787→\n   788→- **2\\. If the [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`\n   789→  (the [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be\n   790→  present):** - No exec-requests are executed but shell-requests are not\n   791→  affected, they follow the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   792→\n   793→- **3\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is present and the\n   794→  [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) value =/= `disabled` (the\n   795→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) may or may not be present):** -\n   796→  The [`exec_spec`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) `fun()` is called with the same\n   797→  number of parameters as the arity of the fun, and the result is returned to\n   798→  the client. Shell-requests are not affected, they follow the\n   799→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value.\n   800→\n   801→- **4\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   802→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with the default Erlang\n   803→  shell as the [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value:** - The\n   804→  default Erlang evaluator is used both for exec and shell requests. The result\n   805→  is returned to the client.\n   806→\n   807→- **5\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   808→  [`shell-option`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`) is present with a value that is\n   809→  neither the default Erlang shell nor the value `disabled`:** - The\n   810→  exec-request is not evaluated and an error message is returned to the client.\n   811→  Shell-requests are executed according to the value of the\n   812→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`).\n   813→\n   814→- **6\\. If the [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) is absent, and the\n   815→  [`shell_spec`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`)'s value is `disabled`:** - Exec\n   816→  requests are executed by the default shell, but shell-requests are not\n   817→  executed.\n   818→\n   819→If a custom CLI is installed (see the option\n   820→[`ssh_cli`](`t:ssh_cli_daemon_option/0`)) the rules above are replaced by thoose\n   821→implied by the custom CLI.\n   822→\n   823→> #### Note {: .info }\n   824→>\n   825→> The [`exec-option`](`t:exec_daemon_option/0`) has existed for a long time but\n   826→> has not previously been documented. The old definition and behaviour are\n   827→> retained but obey the rules 1-6 above if conflicting. The old and undocumented\n   828→> style should not be used in new programs.\n   829→\"\"\".\n   830→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   831→-type exec_result()  :: {ok,Result::term()} | {error,Reason::term()} .\n   832→-doc \"\"\"\n   833→Old-style exec specification that are kept for compatibility, but should not be\n   834→used in new programs\n   835→\"\"\".\n   836→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n   837→-type deprecated_exec_opt() :: fun() | mod_fun_args() .\n   838→\n   839→-doc \"\"\"\n   840→Provides your own CLI implementation in a daemon.\n   841→\n   842→It is a channel callback module that implements a shell and command execution.\n   843→The shell's read-eval-print loop can be customized, using the option\n   844→[`shell`](`t:shell_daemon_option/0`). Sets the text strings that the daemon\n   906→  sends to the client for presentation to the user when using\n   907→  `keyboard-interactive` authentication.\n   908→\n   909→  If the fun/3 or fun/4 is used, it is called when the actual authentication\n   910→  occurs and may therefore return dynamic data like time, remote ip etc.\n   911→\n   912→  The parameter `Echo` guides the client about need to hide the password.\n   913→\n   914→  The default value is:\n   915→  `{auth_method_kb_interactive_data, {\"SSH server\", \"Enter password for \\\"\"++User++\"\\\"\", \"password: \", false}>`\n   916→\n   917→- **`user_passwords`{: #option-user_passwords }** - Provides passwords for\n   918→  password authentication. The passwords are used when someone tries to connect\n   919→  to the server and public key user-authentication fails. The option provides a\n   920→  list of valid usernames and the corresponding passwords.\n   921→\n   922→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n   923→  >\n   924→  > Note that this is very insecure due to the plain-text passwords; it is\n   925→  > intended for test purposes. Use the [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) option\n   926→  > to handle the password checking instead.\n   927→\n   928→- **`pk_check_user`{: #option-pk_check_user }** - Enables checking of the\n   929→  [client's user name](`t:authentication_client_options/0`) in the server when\n   930→  doing public key authentication. It is disabled by default.\n   931→\n   932→  The term \"user\" is used differently in OpenSSH and SSH in Erlang/OTP: see more\n   933→  in the [User's Guide](terminology.md#the-term-user).\n   934→\n   935→  If the option is enabled, and no [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) is present,\n   936→  the user name must present in the\n   937→  [user_passwords](`m:ssh#option-user_passwords`) for the check to succeed but\n   938→  the value of the password is not checked.\n   939→\n   940→  In case of a [`pwdfun`](`m:ssh#option-pwdfun`) checking the user, the atom\n   941→  `pubkey` is put in the password argument.\n   942→\n   943→- **`password`{: #option-password }** - Provides a global password that\n   944→  authenticates any user.\n   945→\n   946→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n   947→  >\n   948→  > Intended to facilitate testing.\n   949→  >\n   950→  > From a security perspective this option makes the server very vulnerable.\n   951→\n   952→- **`pwdfun`{: #option-pwdfun } with `t:pwdfun_4/0`** - Provides a function for\n   953→  password validation. This could used for calling an external system or\n   954→  handling passwords stored as hash values.\n   955→\n   956→  This fun can also be used to make delays in authentication tries for example\n   957→  by calling `timer:sleep/1`.\n   958→\n   959→  To facilitate for instance counting of failed tries, the `State` variable\n   960→  could be used. This state is per connection only. The first time the pwdfun is\n   961→  called for a connection, the `State` variable has the value `undefined`.\n   962→\n   963→  The fun should return:\n   964→\n   965→  - `true` if the user and password is valid\n   966→  - `false` if the user or password is invalid\n   967→  - `disconnect` if a SSH_MSG_DISCONNECT message should be sent immediately. It\n   968→    will be followed by a close of the underlying tcp connection.\n   969→  - `{true, NewState:any()}` if the user and password is valid\n   970→  - `{false, NewState:any()}` if the user or password is invalid\n   971→\n   972→  A third usage is to block login attempts from a missbehaving peer. The `State`\n   973→  described above can be used for this. The return value `disconnect` is useful\n   974→  for this.\n   975→\n   976→  In case of the [`pk_check_user`](`m:ssh#option-pk_check_user`) is set, the\n   977→  atom `pubkey` is put in the password argument when validating a public key\n   978→  login. The pwdfun is then responsible to check that the user name is valid.\n   979→\n   980→- **`pwdfun` with `t:pwdfun_2/0`** - Provides a function for password\n   981→  validation. This function is called with user and password as strings, and\n   982→  returns:\n   983→\n   984→  - `true` if the user and password is valid\n   985→  - `false` if the user or password is invalid\n   986→\n   987→  In case of the [`pk_check_user`](`m:ssh#option-pk_check_user`) is set, the\n   988→  atom `pubkey` is put in the password argument when validating a public key\n   989→  login. The pwdfun is then responsible to check that the user name is valid.\n   990→\n   991→  This variant is kept for compatibility.\n   992→\n   993→- **`no_auth_needed`{: #option-no_auth_needed }** - If `true`, a client is\n   994→  authenticated without any need of providing any password or key.\n   995→\n   996→  This option is only intended for very special applications due to the high\n   997→  risk of accepting any connecting client.\n   998→\n   999→  The default value is `false`.\n  1000→\"\"\".\n  1001→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1002→-type pwdfun_4() :: fun((User::string(),\n  1003→                         Password::string()|pubkey,\n  1004→                         PeerAddress::ip_port(),\n  1005→                         State::any()) ->\n  1006→                               boolean() | disconnect | {boolean(),NewState::any()}\n  1007→                       ) .\n  1008→\n  1009→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1010→-type diffie_hellman_group_exchange_daemon_option() ::\n  1011→        {dh_gex_groups, [explicit_group()] | explicit_group_file() | ssh_moduli_file()}\n  1012→      | {dh_gex_limits, {Min::pos_integer(), Max::pos_integer()} } .\n  1013→\n  1014→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1015→-type explicit_group() :: {Size::pos_integer(),G::pos_integer(),P::pos_integer()} .\n  1016→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1017→-type explicit_group_file() :: {file,string()} .\n  1018→-doc \"\"\"\n  1019→- **`dh_gex_groups`** - Defines the groups the server may choose among when\n  1020→  diffie-hellman-group-exchange is negotiated. See\n  1021→  [RFC 4419](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4419) for details. The three\n  1022→  variants of this option are:\n  1023→\n  1024→  - **`{Size=integer(),G=integer(),P=integer()}`** - The groups are given\n  1025→    explicitly in this list. There may be several elements with the same `Size`.\n  1026→    In such a case, the server will choose one randomly in the negotiated Size.\n  1027→\n  1028→  - **`{file,filename()}`** - The file must have one or more three-tuples\n  1029→    `{Size=integer(),G=integer(),P=integer()}` terminated by a dot. The file is\n  1030→    read when the daemon starts.\n  1031→\n  1032→  - **`{ssh_moduli_file,filename()}`** - The file must be in\n  1033→    [ssh-keygen moduli file format](`public_key:dh_gex_group/4`). The file is\n  1034→    read when the daemon starts.\n  1035→\n  1036→  The default list is fetched from the [public_key](`public_key:dh_gex_group/4`)\n  1037→  application.\n  1038→\n  1039→- **`dh_gex_limits`** - Limits what a client can ask for in\n  1040→  diffie-hellman-group-exchange. The limits will be\n  1041→  `{MaxUsed = min(MaxClient,Max), MinUsed = max(MinClient,Min)}` where\n  1042→  `MaxClient` and `MinClient` are the values proposed by a connecting client.\n  1043→\n  1044→  The default value is `{0,infinity}`.\n  1045→\n  1046→  If `MaxUsed < MinUsed` in a key exchange, it will fail with a disconnect.\n  1047→\n  1048→  See [RFC 4419](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4419) for the function of the\n  1049→  Max and Min values.\n  1050→\"\"\".\n  1051→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1052→-type ssh_moduli_file() :: {ssh_moduli_file,string()}.\n  1053→\n  1054→-doc \"\"\"\n  1055→Maximum time in milliseconds for the first channel start after completion of the\n  1056→authentication negotiation. Thus, if set to `N`, and `N`\n  1094→  clients have connected but not started the login process, connection attempt\n  1095→  `N+1` is aborted. If `N` connections are authenticated and still logged in, no\n  1096→  more logins are accepted until one of the existing ones log out.\n  1097→\n  1098→  The counter is per listening port. Thus, if two daemons are started, one with\n  1099→  `{max_sessions,N}` and the other with `{max_sessions,M}`, in total `N+M`\n  1100→  connections are accepted for the whole `ssh` application.\n  1101→\n  1102→  Notice that if `parallel_login` is `false`, only one client at a time can be\n  1103→  in the authentication phase.\n  1104→\n  1105→  By default, this option is not set. This means that the number is not limited.\n  1106→\n  1107→- **`max_channels`{: #hardening_daemon_options-max_channels }** - The maximum\n  1108→  number of channels with active remote subsystem that are accepted for each\n  1109→  connection to this daemon\n  1110→\n  1111→  By default, this option is not set. This means that the number is not limited.\n  1112→\n  1113→- **`parallel_login`{: #hardening_daemon_options-parallel_login }** - If set to\n  1114→  false (the default value), only one login is handled at a time. If set to\n  1115→  true, an unlimited number of login attempts are allowed simultaneously.\n  1116→\n  1117→  If the `max_sessions` option is set to `N` and `parallel_login` is set to\n  1118→  `true`, the maximum number of simultaneous login attempts at any time is\n  1119→  limited to `N-K`, where `K` is the number of authenticated connections present\n  1120→  at this daemon.\n  1121→\n  1122→  > #### Warning {: .warning }\n  1123→  >\n  1124→  > Do not enable `parallel_logins` without protecting the server by other\n  1125→  > means, for example, by the `max_sessions` option or a firewall\n  1126→  > configuration. If set to `true`, there is no protection against DOS attacks.\n  1127→\n  1128→- **`minimal_remote_max_packet_size`{:\n  1129→  #hardening_daemon_options-minimal_remote_max_packet_size }** - The least\n  1130→  maximum packet size that the daemon will accept in channel open requests from\n  1131→  the client. The default value is 0.\n  1132→\"\"\".\n  1133→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1134→-type hardening_daemon_options() ::\n  1135→        {max_sessions, pos_integer()}\n  1136→      | {max_channels, pos_integer()}\n  1137→      | {parallel_login, boolean()}\n  1138→      | {minimal_remote_max_packet_size, pos_integer()}.\n  1139→\n  1140→-doc \"\"\"\n  1141→- **`connectfun`** - Provides a fun to implement your own logging when a user\n  1142→  authenticates to the server.\n  1143→\n  1144→- **`failfun`** - Provides a fun to implement your own logging when a user fails\n  1145→  to authenticate.\n  1146→\"\"\".\n  1147→-doc(#{title => <<\"Daemon Options\">>}).\n  1148→-type callbacks_daemon_options() ::\n  1149→        {failfun, fun((User::string(), Peer::{inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()}, Reason::term()) -> _)}\n  1150→      | {connectfun, fun((User::string(), Peer::{inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()}, Method::string()) ->_)} .\n  1151→\n  1152→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1153→-type opaque_daemon_options()  ::\n  1154→        {infofun, fun()}\n  1155→      | opaque_common_options().\n  1156→\n  1157→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1158→-type ip_port() :: {inet:ip_address(), inet:port_number()} .\n  1159→\n  1160→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1161→-type mod_args() :: {Module::atom(), Args::list()} .\n  1162→-doc(#{title => <<\"Other data types\">>}).\n  1163→-type mod_fun_args() :: {Module::atom(), Function::atom(), Args::list()} .\n  1164→\n  1165→\n  1166→%% Records\n  1167→-record(address, {address,\n  1168→                  port,\n  1169→                  profile\n  1170→                 }).\n  1171→\n  1172→-record(ssh,\n  1173→\t{\n  1174→\t  role :: role(),\n  1175→\t  peer :: undefined | \n  1176→                  {inet:hostname(),ip_port()},         %% string version of peer address \n  1177→\n  1178→          local,        %% Local sockname. KEY DATA STRUCTURES FOR SECURITY ANALYSIS\n\n**SSH Connection State (ssh.hrl:1172-1250):**\n```erlang\n-record(ssh,\n    {\n        role :: role(),        %% client | server\n        peer,                  %% Remote peer info\n        authenticated = false, %% CRITICAL security flag\n        user,                  %% Authenticated user\n        service,               %% Service name\n        ...\n        send_sequence,         %% Packet sequence numbers\n        recv_sequence,\n        ...\n    }).\n```\n\n**Channel Record (ssh_connect.hrl:240-261):**\n```erlang\n-record(channel,\n    {\n        type,                  %% \"session\"\n        sys,                   %% \"none\", \"shell\", \"exec\" \"subsystem\"\n        user,                  %% User process id\n        local_id,              %% Local channel id\n        remote_id,             %% Remote channel id\n        recv_window_size,      %% Flow control\n        send_window_size,\n        ...\n    }).\n```\n\n**Connection Record (ssh_connect.hrl:263-273):**\n```erlang\n-record(connection, {\n    requests = [],           %% Pending requests\n    channel_cache,           %% Active channels\n    channel_id_seed,         %% ID generation\n    cli_spec,                %% CLI module\n    options,                 %% Server options\n    exec,                    %% Exec function\n    connection_supervisor    %% Supervising process\n}).\n```\n\n---\n\n### 8. CRITICAL EXECUTION PATHS FOR COMMAND EXECUTION\n\n```\nClient sends SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN (type 90)\n  ↓\nssh_connection_handler decrypts packet (1135-1192)\n  ↓\nssh_message:decode() creates #ssh_msg_channel_open{}\n  ↓\nssh_connection:handle_msg() dispatches (759-801)\n  ↓\nssh_connection:setup_session() creates #channel{} record (1407-1431)\n  ↓\nCLI module started (ssh_connection:start_cli - 1437-1446)\n  ↓\nClient sends SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST (type 98) with \"exec\"\n  ↓\nssh_connection:handle_msg() routes to exec handler (1147-1154)\n  ↓\nhandle_cli_msg() forwards to SSH CLI module (1802+)\n  ↓\nssh_cli:handle_ssh_msg() receives {exec, ...} message (194-242)\n  ↓\nexec_direct() OR exec_in_erlang_default_shell() OR start_exec_shell()\n  ↓\nexec_in_self_group() spawns execution process (821-852)\n  ↓\nFun() executes (either user function, Erlang eval, or shell)\n  ↓\nResult sent back through SSH channel\n```\n\n---\n\nThis analysis provides a comprehensive understanding of the SSH server's architecture for security vulnerability assessment. This document covers the architecture and key components of the SSH server implementation.\n\n## SSH Protocol Layers\n\nThe SSH protocol consists of three layers:\n1. **Transport Layer** (RFC 4253) - Provides server authentication, confidentiality, and integrity\n2. **User Authentication Protocol** (RFC 4252) - Authenticates the client to the server\n3. **Connection Protocol** (RFC 4254) - Multiplexes the encrypted tunnel into logical channels\n\n## Key Source Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `ssh_connection_handler.erl` | Main gen_statem state machine handling connection lifecycle |\n| `ssh_connection.erl` | SSH connection protocol (channels, requests) |\n| `ssh_transport.erl` | Transport layer (encryption, MAC, key exchange) |\n| `ssh_auth.erl` | User authentication logic |\n| `ssh_message.erl` | Protocol message encoding/decoding |\n| `ssh_cli.erl` | Shell and exec command execution |\n\n## Header Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `ssh.hrl` | Core data structures, #ssh{} record, constants |\n| `ssh_connect.hrl` | Connection protocol message types (90-100) |\n| `ssh_auth.hrl` | Authentication message types (50-60) |\n| `ssh_transport.hrl` | Transport message types (1-49) |\n\n## SSH Message Types (RFC 4254 - Connection Protocol)\n\n```erlang\n%% Channel messages (from ssh_connect.hrl)\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN,                         90).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION,            91).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_FAILURE,                 92).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_WINDOW_ADJUST,                93).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA,                         94).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EXTENDED_DATA,                95).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EOF,                          96).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_CLOSE,                        97).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST,                      98).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS,                      99).\n-define(SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE,                     100).\n```\n\n## SSH Channel Request Types\n\nChannel requests (message type 98) support various operations:\n\n| Request Type | Purpose |\n|-------------|---------|\n| `\"shell\"` | Request interactive shell |\n| `\"exec\"` | Execute command |\n| `\"subsystem\"` | Start subsystem (e.g., SFTP) |\n| `\"env\"` | Set environment variable |\n| `\"pty-req\"` | Allocate pseudo-terminal |\n| `\"signal\"` | Send signal to process |\n| `\"window-change\"` | Terminal window size change |\n\n## Core Data Structures\n\n### #ssh{} Record (ssh.hrl)\n\n```erlang\n-record(ssh, {\n    role :: client | server,\n    peer,                      %% Remote peer info\n    authenticated = false,     %% Authentication status\n    user,                      %% Authenticated user\n    service,                   %% Current service\n    userauth_methods,          %% Available auth methods\n    send_sequence = 0,         %% Packet sequence numbers\n    recv_sequence = 0,\n    %% Encryption state\n    encrypt,\n    decrypt,\n    %% MAC state\n    send_mac,\n    recv_mac,\n    %% ... more fields\n}).\n```\n\n### #channel{} Record (ssh_connect.hrl)\n\n```erlang\n-record(channel, {\n    type,              %% Channel type (usually \"session\")\n    sys,               %% \"shell\" | \"exec\" | \"subsystem\"\n    user,              %% User process pid\n    local_id,          %% Local channel ID\n    remote_id,         %% Remote channel ID\n    recv_window_size,  %% Flow control\n    send_window_size,\n    sent_close = false\n}).\n```\n\n### #connection{} Record (ssh_connect.hrl)\n\n```erlang\n-record(connection, {\n    requests = [],           %% Pending requests\n    channel_cache,           %% ets table of channels\n    channel_id_seed,         %% Next channel ID\n    cli_spec,                %% CLI callback module\n    options,                 %% Server options\n    exec,                    %% Exec callback\n    connection_supervisor    %% Supervisor pid\n}).\n```\n\n## Message Dispatch Flow\n\n```\nTCP Socket\n    ↓\nssh_connection_handler (handle_event/info)\n    ↓\nssh_transport:handle_packet_part() - decrypt\n    ↓\nssh_message:decode() - parse binary to record\n    ↓\nPattern match on message type\n    ↓\nssh_connection:handle_msg() - for connection protocol\n    ↓\nChannel request handler\n    ↓\nssh_cli module - for shell/exec\n```\n\n## Channel Request Handling in ssh_connection.erl\n\n```erlang\n%% Handle exec request\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    request_type = \"exec\",\n                                    want_reply = WantReply,\n                                    data = Data},\n           Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n                   {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n\n%% Handle shell request\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    request_type = \"shell\",\n                                    want_reply = WantReply},\n           Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n                   {shell, ChannelId, WantReply});\n```\n\n## Command Execution Paths in ssh_cli.erl\n\n1. **Direct Function Execution**: User-provided callback function\n2. **Erlang Expression Evaluation**: Default - evaluates Erlang expressions\n3. **Legacy Shell**: Uses Erlang shell with I/O redirection\n\n## Security Considerations\n\n1. **Authentication State**: The `#ssh.authenticated` flag indicates successful authentication\n2. **State Machine**: Connection must be in `{connected, Role}` state for channel operations\n3. **Channel Validation**: Channels must exist in cache before operations\n4. **Command Execution**: Commands are executed with server process privileges\n\n## Example: SSH Session Lifecycle\n\n```\n1. TCP connection established\n2. SSH version exchange\n3. Key exchange (kex)\n4. Service request (\"ssh-userauth\")\n5. User authentication (password/publickey/etc.)\n6. SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS → state becomes {connected, server}\n7. Client opens channel (SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN)\n8. Client requests exec/shell (SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST)\n9. The vulnerability arises from improper handling of SSH connection protocol messages before user authentication is complete.\n\n### Root Cause\n\nThe SSH protocol specifies a strict sequence:\n1. Transport layer establishment\n2. User authentication\n3. Connection protocol (channels, requests)\n\nThe vulnerability occurs when connection protocol messages (SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST) are processed before authentication completes.\n\n### Affected Code Location\n\nIn `ssh_connection_handler.erl`, the message dispatch code:\n\n```erlang\n%% Lines ~1161-1177 - Message dispatch without state check\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0) ->\n    %% ... packet decryption ...\n    try ssh_message:decode(...) of\n        %% These messages dispatched regardless of StateName!\n        #ssh_msg_channel_open{}    = Msg -> {keep_state, D1, ?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)};\n        #ssh_msg_channel_request{} = Msg -> {keep_state, D1, ?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)};\n        %% ... more connection messages ...\n```\n\nThe `?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)` macro dispatches these to the `{conn_msg, Msg}` handler:\n\n```erlang\n%% Line 759 - Handler accepts conn_msg in ANY state\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{...} = D0) ->\n    %% No check that StateName == {connected, _}!\n    try ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, Connection0, Role, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n```\n\n### Attack Vector\n\nAn attacker can:\n1. Establish TCP connection to SSH server\n2. Complete SSH version exchange\n3. Complete key exchange (kex)\n4. Skip authentication entirely\n5. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN to create a channel\n6. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with type \"exec\" containing arbitrary command\n7. Command executes with server privileges\n\n### Exploitation Example (Conceptual)\n\n```python\n# Pseudo-code for attack\ndef exploit_ssh(target):\n    sock = connect(target, 22)\n\n    # Version exchange\n    send_version(sock, \"SSH-2.0-OpenSSH_8.0\")\n    recv_version(sock)\n\n    # Key exchange\n    perform_kex(sock)\n\n    # Skip SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST for ssh-userauth\n    # Skip authentication entirely\n\n    # Directly send channel open\n    send_channel_open(sock, channel_id=0, type=\"session\")\n    recv_channel_open_confirmation(sock)\n\n    # Send exec request with malicious command\n    send_channel_request(sock, channel_id=0,\n                         type=\"exec\",\n                         command=\"id; cat /etc/passwd\")\n\n    # Receive command output\n    output = recv_channel_data(sock)\n    print(output)  # Command executed!\n```\n\n## Security Patterns in Erlang/OTP SSH\n\n### Pattern 1: State Machine Guards\n\nSecure handlers use pattern matching or guards on state:\n\n```erlang\n%% Good: Explicit state check in function head\nhandle_event({call,From}, {close, ChannelId}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% Only executed when connected\n\n%% Good: Using CONNECTED macro\n-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n        (element(1,StateName) == connected orelse\n         element(1,StateName) == ext_info)).\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Catch-All Rejection\n\nUnhandled states should reject operations:\n\n```erlang\n%% For operations requiring authentication\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName, D)\n  when not ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% Reject with protocol error or disconnect\n    {Shutdown, D1} = send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR,\n                                     \"Connection message before authentication\",\n                                     StateName, D),\n    {stop, Shutdown, D1};\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Authentication Flag Check\n\nAdditional defense-in-depth using the authenticated flag:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName,\n             #data{ssh_params = #ssh{authenticated = false}} = D) ->\n    %% Reject unauthenticated connection messages\n    ...\n```\n\n## Fix Strategy\n\nThe fix must ensure connection protocol messages are only processed after successful authentication:\n\n1. **Primary Fix**: Add state guard to `{conn_msg, Msg}` handler\n2. **Secondary Fix**: Add guard at message dispatch point\n3. **Defense in Depth**: Check `authenticated` flag in #ssh{} record\n\n### Recommended Fix (Primary)\n\nModify the conn_msg handler to require connected state:\n\n```erlang\n%% Add guard to only process when connected\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{...} = D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% ... existing handler code ...\n\n%% Add rejection handler for other states\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, _Msg}, StateName, D) ->\n    {Shutdown, D1} = send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR,\n                                     \"Unexpected connection protocol message\",\n                                     StateName, D),\n    {stop, Shutdown, D1}.\n```\n\n### Alternative Fix (At Dispatch Point)\n\nOnly dispatch connection messages when in connected state:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% ... existing dispatch code for connection messages ...\n\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0) ->\n    %% When not connected, reject connection protocol messages\n    case ssh_message:decode(...) of\n        Msg when is_record(Msg, ssh_msg_channel_open);\n                 is_record(Msg, ssh_msg_channel_request);\n                 %% ... other connection messages ...\n            ->\n            send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, ...);\n        Msg ->\n            %% Handle other messages normally\n            ...\n    end.\n```\n\n## Testing the Fix\n\n### Positive Tests (Should Still Work)\n1. Normal SSH connection with password auth\n2. Normal SSH connection with public key auth\n3. Channel open after authentication\n4. Exec command after authentication\n5. Shell session after authentication\n6. SFTP subsystem after authentication\n\n### Negative Tests (Should Be Blocked)\n1. Channel open before authentication → disconnect\n2. Channel request before authentication → disconnect\n3. Exec request before authentication → disconnect\n4. Shell request before authentication → disconnect\n\n## Related CVEs and Vulnerabilities\n\n- **CVE-2025-32433**: Pre-auth RCE in Erlang/OTP SSH (this vulnerability)\n- Similar patterns exist in other SSH implementations (libssh, etc.)\n\n## Security Recommendations\n\n1. Always validate state machine position before processing sensitive operations\n2. Use explicit guards rather than relying on message ordering\n3. Implement defense-in-depth with multiple validation layers\n4. Log and disconnect on protocol violations\n5. Review all message handlers for state requirements\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":305,"cache_read_input_tokens":37402,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":305,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ba3bc776-d8a6-470a-b9d5-ee0d2588e2e3","uuid":"674fddfc-f8d0-4e6b-915d-a8913a4d74b8"}

Command executes with server privileges\n\n### Exploitation Example (Conceptual)\n\n```python\n# Pseudo-code for attack\ndef exploit_ssh(target):\n    sock = connect(target, 22)\n\n    # Version exchange\n    send_version(sock, \"SSH-2.0-OpenSSH_8.0\")\n    recv_version(sock)\n\n    # Key exchange\n    perform_kex(sock)\n\n    # Skip SSH_MSG_SERVICE_REQUEST for ssh-userauth\n    # Skip authentication entirely\n\n    # Directly send channel open\n    send_channel_open(sock, channel_id=0, type=\"session\")\n    recv_channel_open_confirmation(sock)\n\n    # Send exec request with malicious command\n    send_channel_request(sock, channel_id=0,\n                         type=\"exec\",\n                         command=\"id; cat /etc/passwd\")\n\n    # Receive command output\n    output = recv_channel_data(sock)\n    print(output)  # Command executed!\n```\n\n## Security Patterns in Erlang/OTP SSH\n\n### Pattern 1: State Machine Guards\n\nSecure handlers use pattern matching or guards on state:\n\n```erlang\n%% Good: Explicit state check in function head\nhandle_event({call,From}, {close, ChannelId}, StateName, D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% Only executed when connected\n\n%% Good: Using CONNECTED macro\n-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n        (element(1,StateName) == connected orelse\n         element(1,StateName) == ext_info)).\n```\n\n### Pattern 2: Catch-All Rejection\n\nUnhandled states should reject operations:\n\n```erlang\n%% For operations requiring authentication\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName, D)\n  when not ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% Reject with protocol error or disconnect\n    {Shutdown, D1} = send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR,\n                                     \"Connection message before authentication\",\n                                     StateName, D),\n    {stop, Shutdown, D1};\n```\n\n### Pattern 3: Authentication Flag Check\n\nAdditional defense-in-depth using the authenticated flag:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName,\n             #data{ssh_params = #ssh{authenticated = false}} = D) ->\n    %% Reject unauthenticated connection messages\n    ...\n```\n\n## Fix Strategy\n\nThe fix must ensure connection protocol messages are only processed after successful authentication:\n\n1. **Primary Fix**: Add state guard to `{conn_msg, Msg}` handler\n2. **Secondary Fix**: Add guard at message dispatch point\n3. **Defense in Depth**: Check `authenticated` flag in #ssh{} record\n\n### Recommended Fix (Primary)\n\nModify the conn_msg handler to require connected state:\n\n```erlang\n%% Add guard to only process when connected\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{...} = D0)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% ... existing handler code ...\n\n%% Add rejection handler for other states\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, _Msg}, StateName, D) ->\n    {Shutdown, D1} = send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR,\n                                     \"Unexpected connection protocol message\",\n                                     StateName, D),\n    {stop, Shutdown, D1}.\n```\n\n### Alternative Fix (At Dispatch Point)\n\nOnly dispatch connection messages when in connected state:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0) when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% ... existing dispatch code for connection messages ...\n\nhandle_event(info, {Proto, Sock, NewData}, StateName, D0) ->\n    %% When not connected, reject connection protocol messages\n    case ssh_message:decode(...) of\n        Msg when is_record(Msg, ssh_msg_channel_open);\n                 is_record(Msg, ssh_msg_channel_request);\n                 %% ... other connection messages ...\n            ->\n            send_disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, ...);\n        Msg ->\n            %% Handle other messages normally\n            ...\n    end.\n```\n\n## Testing the Fix\n\n### Positive Tests (Should Still Work)\n1. Normal SSH connection with password auth\n2. Normal SSH connection with public key auth\n3. Channel open after authentication\n4. Exec command after authentication\n5. Shell session after authentication\n6. SFTP subsystem after authentication\n\n### Negative Tests (Should Be Blocked)\n1. Channel open before authentication → disconnect\n2. Channel request before authentication → disconnect\n3. Exec request before authentication → disconnect\n4. Shell request before authentication → disconnect\n\n## Related CVEs and Vulnerabilities\n\n- **CVE-2025-32433**: Pre-auth RCE in Erlang/OTP SSH (this vulnerability)\n- Similar patterns exist in other SSH implementations (libssh, etc.)\n\n## Security Recommendations\n\n1. Always validate state machine position before processing sensitive operations\n2. Use explicit guards rather than relying on message ordering\n3. Implement defense-in-depth with multiple validation layers\n4. Log and disconnect on protocol violations\n5. Review all message handlers for state requirements\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

It replaces the older `gen_fsm` and provides more flexibility for handling events in different states.\n\n## Callback Modes\n\n```erlang\ncallback_mode() ->\n    [handle_event_function,   %% Use handle_event/4 for all events\n     state_enter].            %% Call handle_event on state entry\n```\n\n### handle_event_function Mode\n\nAll events are handled by a single callback:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(EventType, EventContent, State, Data) ->\n    {next_state, NewState, NewData} |\n    {next_state, NewState, NewData, Actions} |\n    {keep_state, NewData} |\n    {keep_state, NewData, Actions} |\n    keep_state_and_data |\n    {stop, Reason, NewData} |\n    {stop_and_reply, Reason, Replies, NewData}.\n```\n\n## Event Types\n\n| EventType | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| `{call, From}` | Synchronous call with reply |\n| `cast` | Asynchronous message |\n| `info` | Regular Erlang message |\n| `internal` | Internal event (from actions) |\n| `enter` | State entry (with state_enter mode) |\n| `{timeout, Name}` | Named timeout |\n| `timeout` | Generic timeout |\n\n## State Pattern Matching\n\n### Using Guards for State Validation\n\n```erlang\n%% Handle event only in specific state\nhandle_event({call, From}, get_data, {connected, _Role}, Data) ->\n    {keep_state_and_data, [{reply, From, Data}]};\n\n%% Handle in any state\nhandle_event({call, From}, stop, _State, Data) ->\n    {stop_and_reply, normal, [{reply, From, ok}], Data}.\n```\n\n### Using Macros for State Checks\n\n```erlang\n-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n        (element(1, StateName) == connected orelse\n         element(1, StateName) == ext_info)).\n\nhandle_event({call, From}, {close, Id}, StateName, D)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    %% Only executed when in connected or ext_info state\n    ...\n```\n\n## Actions\n\nActions are returned in a list with the state transition:\n\n```erlang\n{keep_state, NewData, [\n    {reply, From, Response},        %% Reply to caller\n    {next_event, internal, Event},  %% Queue internal event\n    {timeout, Time, Msg},           %% Set timeout\n    {{timeout, Name}, Time, Msg}    %% Named timeout\n]}.\n```\n\n### next_event Action\n\nQueue an event to be processed immediately:\n\n```erlang\n%% Queue internal event for later processing\n{keep_state, D, [{next_event, internal, {process, Data}}]}\n```\n\n## SSH Connection Handler State Machine\n\nThe Erlang SSH server uses gen_statem with these states:\n\n```\nStates:\n- {hello, client | server}           - Version exchange\n- {kex_init, client | server}        - Key exchange init\n- {kex_dh_*, client | server}        - DH key exchange phases\n- {service_request, client | server} - Service request\n- {userauth, client | server}        - User authentication\n- {connected, client | server}       - Authenticated and connected\n- {ext_info, Role, renegotiate}      - Extension info during rekey\n```\n\n### State Transitions\n\n```\n{hello, _}\n    ↓ version exchange complete\n{kex_init, _}\n    ↓ kex init complete\n{kex_dh_*, _}\n    ↓ key exchange complete\n{service_request, _}\n    ↓ service request for ssh-userauth\n{userauth, _}\n    ↓ SSH_MSG_USERAUTH_SUCCESS\n{connected, _}   ← Only state where channels work\n    ↓ rekey\n{ext_info, _, renegotiate}\n    ↓\n{connected, _}\n```\n\n## Security-Critical Pattern: State Guards\n\nFor security-sensitive operations, always use state guards:\n\n```erlang\n%% INSECURE: Accepts message in any state\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName, D) ->\n    process_connection_message(Msg, D).\n\n%% SECURE: Only accepts in connected state\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName, D)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    process_connection_message(Msg, D);\n\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, _Msg}, _StateName, D) ->\n    %% Reject in non-connected states\n    disconnect_with_error(D).\n```\n\n## Internal Events Pattern\n\nCommon pattern for processing decoded messages:\n\n```erlang\n%% Define macro to queue connection message\n-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n        [{next_event, internal, prepare_next_packet},\n         {next_event, internal, {conn_msg, Msg}}]).\n\n%% In message decode handler\nhandle_event(info, {tcp, Sock, Data}, StateName, D) ->\n    case decode_message(Data) of\n        #ssh_msg_channel_request{} = Msg ->\n            {keep_state, D1, ?CONNECTION_MSG(Msg)};\n        ...\n    end.\n\n%% Internal event handler processes the message\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, StateName, D)\n  when ?CONNECTED(StateName) ->\n    ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, ...);\n```\n\n## Error Handling Pattern\n\n```erlang\nhandle_event(EventType, Event, State, Data) ->\n    try\n        process_event(Event, State, Data)\n    catch\n        Class:Reason:Stack ->\n            log_error(Class, Reason, Stack),\n            {stop, {error, Reason}, Data}\n    end.\n```\n\n## Timeout Patterns\n\n```erlang\n%% Set timeout on state entry\nhandle_event(enter, _OldState, {waiting, _}, Data) ->\n    {keep_state, Data, [{timeout, 30000, wait_timeout}]};\n\n%% Handle timeout\nhandle_event(timeout, wait_timeout, {waiting, _}, Data) ->\n    {stop, timeout, Data}.\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use guards for security-sensitive operations** - Don't rely on message ordering\n2. **Define state check macros** - Centralize state validation logic\n3. **Handle unexpected events** - Catch-all clauses for unknown states\n4. **Log protocol violations** - Security audit trail\n5. **Use internal events** - Decouple message decoding from processing\n6. **Return appropriate disconnect** - Use protocol error codes correctly\n\n## Example: Secure Event Handler\n\n```erlang\n-define(CONNECTED(S), (element(1,S) == connected orelse element(1,S) == ext_info)).\n\n%% Connection messages only in authenticated state\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, Msg}, State, D) when ?CONNECTED(State) ->\n    process_connection_msg(Msg, D);\n\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg, _Msg}, State, D) ->\n    %% Protocol violation - reject\n    disconnect(?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR,\n               \"Connection protocol message before authentication\",\n               State, D);\n\n%% Authentication messages only in userauth state\nhandle_event(internal, #ssh_msg_userauth_request{} = Msg, {userauth, server}, D) ->\n    process_auth_request(Msg, D);\n\n%% Catch-all for unexpected messages\nhandle_event(EventType, Event, State, D) ->\n    log_unexpected_event(EventType, Event, State),\n    keep_state_and_data.\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":305,"cache_read_input_tokens":39683,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":305,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ba3bc776-d8a6-470a-b9d5-ee0d2588e2e3","uuid":"6299b146-be7a-425d-9754-6ed34c907568"}

receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {error,    stop(ConnPid),\n            {error, {exit,Reason}}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end;\nhandshake(ConnPid, client, Ref, Timeout) ->\n    receive\n\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n\t Reason}\n    after Timeout ->\n\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n\t    {error, timeout}\n    end.\n\nhandshake(Msg, #data{starter = User}) ->\n
